### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on final JMTBA orders, which we believe
could be among the catalysts for Airtac's and Hiwin's share
prices.
Per the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline orders grew
2% MoM and 65% YoY in August 2026 (vs. -5% MoM/+50% YoY in July 2026), to
¥198bn.
• Domestic orders  -3% MoM/+62% YoY (vs.-8% MoM/+50% YoY     in July), to
¥52bn
• Overseas orders  +5% MoM/+66% YoY (vs. -4% MoM/+51% YoY   in July), to

¥146bn
• By region:
° China: -2% MoM/+62% YoY, to ¥47bn
° Europe: -12% MoM/+44% YoY, to ¥20bn
° North America: +24% MoM/+72% YoY, to ¥50bn
Our view:
• We think the solid YoY growth for overseas orders continues to point to an
upcycle scenario, despite some seasonal volatility  with MoM growth in some
regions,
• On top of overall segment recovery within automation:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should benefit from its own share gains and
incremental contributions from linear motion components. Valuation

appears undemanding at 17x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average since 2020).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show margin expansion in the coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside as the stock now trades at 29x 2027e P/E
(vs. peak cycle valuation of 35-40x).
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
September 30, 2026 05:11 PM GMT

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 076: 261005_daiwa_GUC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_GUC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Global Unichip (GUC) reported September revenue which closed its 3Q26 top-
line 19% above market expectations thanks to its stronger-than-expected turnkey revenue 
run-rate during the quarter, likely attributable to more capacity support from its key foundry 
helping GUC meet strong demand from customers for AI projects. We see upside risk to 
our/consensus 3Q26 bottom-line estimates but are currently comfortable with our 4Q26 
forecasts despite the 3Q26 revenue beat likely dwarfing QoQ growth for 4Q26. We have a 
Buy (1) rating on the counter and will review our model in due course post its quarterly 
results release.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 top-line beating expectations. GUC released September revenue of TWD7.1bn, 
up 22% MoM (+98% YoY, see chart below), which closed its 3Q26 top-line at 
TWD18.8bn, up 35% QoQ (+118% YoY) for a new record. 3Q26 revenue beat our and 
consensus estimates both by 19% each thanks to its turnkey business (ie, mass-
production) which contributed TWD16.7bn for 89% of total and beat our estimate by 
22%. We attribute the beat to likely its key foundry continuously unleashing extra 
capacity helping GUC meet the strong customer AI demand during the quarter.

• Quarterly results preview and outlook. We expect GUC to have earned 3Q26 net 
profit of TWD1.7bn, varying not much from the consensus estimate, yet given the top-
line beat, we now see upside risk to the market expectations. As for 4Q26, our top-line 
and bottom-line forecasts are currently 10% and 26% above the consensus, 
respectively, making us comfortable with our current forecasts since we see the 3Q26 
beat being driven by increased foundry capacity allocation for some shipment pull-in, 
thus dwarfing 4Q26’s QoQ growth on intact AI demand. As flagged in our previous note 
(Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to growth, 5 August 2026), we continue to 
expect GUC to be a key beneficiary from the strong AI ASIC ramps, including AI CPU 
and accelerators.

Recommendation 
We rate GUC Buy (1) with a 12M TP of TWD 5,125 on our 4-quarter forward PER target of 
63x. The stock performed well recently which we see as largely pricing in the 3Q26 top-line 
beat, therefore only suggesting investors accumulate only on any technical pullback. Key 
downside risks are less foundry support and margin execution.

Country

## Document 091: 261006_ml_kingslide

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_ml_kingslide.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

King Slide

Maintain Buy: Sep sales -31% MoM from 
high bases; Aug earnings remain solid

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 14,888 TWD | Price: 13,100 TWD

Sep sales -31% MoM from high bases; 3Q26 sales in-line 
King Slide’s Sep sales of NT$4.46bn declined 31% MoM (+196% YoY), after the strong 
sales in the past few months (up 17-46% MoM in Apr-Jul and up 1% MoM in Aug). 3Q26 
sales of NT$17.34bn (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) were 0.5% above BofAe of NT$17.26bn 
and 0.9% below consensus of NT$17.49bn.  
We are not surprised with the MoM sales drop in Sep as (1) the company’s MoM sales 
trend has been volatile (Exhibit 1 and Exhibit 2), (2) sales bases in Apr-Aug were at 
record levels, (3) contribution from Nvidia Rubin GPU projects should be more 
meaningful in 4Q26 after hardware components ramp-up, and (4) partial general server 
orders/demand likely were pushed out due to key component shortage (e.g. memory, 
CPU). 
Solid Aug EPS NT$40.07; pre-tax/net margin 74.6%/59.1% 
In addition to Sep sales, on the same day (6 Oct), King Slide also announced its Aug 
unaudited key financial data as required by local regulations (Exhibit 3). Aug sales were 
NT$6,466mn (+344% YoY), while pre-tax profit was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY) and net 
income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY). EPS came in at NT$40.07 (+212% YoY). Pre-tax 
margin and net margin were 74.6% and 59.1%, respectively.  
Compared to July unaudited figures announced on 14 Aug (see our 17 Aug report), 
including sales of NT$6,407mn, pre-tax margin of 93.4%, net margin of 74.7%, and EPS 
of NT$50.24, we believe the margin and EPS differences mainly reflected FX impact 
under similar sales scale. 
Combining Jul and Aug unaudited results, sales totaled NT$12.87bn and EPS reached 
NT$90.31. Pre-tax margin and net margin were 84.0% and 66.9%, respectively. Jul + Aug 
sales achieved 75%/74% of BofAe/consensus 3Q26 sales. Pre-tax profit, net income, 
and EPS all achieved 79% of BofAe (79.4% pre-tax margin, 62.9% net margin, and 
NT$113.87 EPS) and 77-78% of consensus (80.3% pre-tax margin, 63.1% net margin, 
and NT$117.62 EPS). We also noted that Jul + Aug EPS of NT$90.31 already surpassed 
EPS of NT$74.38 in 2Q26 with similar pre-tax/net margin (84.0%/66.9% in Jul + Aug, vs. 
84.2%/65.4% in 2Q26). 
Maintain Buy; expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction 
We expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction in the near term in view of significant 
MoM sales decline in Sep but also still solid Aug unaudited data. We maintain Buy on 
King Slide as the server rail market leader with broad and diversified client/project base. 
We expect the company to post 3Q26 results after market close on 12 Nov (Thu).

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$14,888 is based on 32x 2027E EPS. We use 32x P/E multiple (2.5SD 
above P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading 
position in server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 
2026-28E. Its early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much 
larger scale than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins. 
Its above-30x P/E is a similar case to other component sub-sector leaders in Taiwan.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

由於北美 Honda 新車型訂單貢獻顯著，帶動 3Q26 營收淡季不淡，加上
電子新業務的訂單開始貢獻，為豐祥下半年營運挹注強勁成長動能， 研
究部據此上修豐祥今年獲利 8%，預估2026 年 EPS 17.17 元(+41%YOY)。
展望 2027 年，預期在休閒車輛、電動機車新訂單以及電子新業務貢獻
下，豐祥明年業績將繼續攀高，預估 2027 年 EPS 21.09 元。研究部給
予 12 倍本益比，將目標價上調至 250 元，維持買進評等。

重點摘要：

1. 電子業務啟動第二成長曲線，2027年進入量產階段： 
豐祥將電子業務視為企業新一輪發展的核心驅動力 ， 當前以AI
伺服器機櫃為主力產品以及其他電子相關品項。 今年下半年已
陸續有新產品少量出貨。在營收比重部分，2026年電子業務約
占合併營收的3~5%。未來隨著客戶需求釋放及新項目量產，搭
配新廠房啟用，創造更多生產空間，公司規畫2H27開始以大規
模自動化模式提升產能， 預期2027年電子業務將有更多的營收
貢獻，更能增添中長期營運動能。。

2. 3Q26淡季不淡，4Q26營收再攀高，年度獲利上修： 
豐祥第三季繼續受惠北美Honda新專案出貨順暢，季度營收 表
現優於原先季減的預期， 呈現淡季不淡 ， 延續前季的強勁表現 ，
年增率保持大幅成長 ； 之後進入越南當地電動機車零件拉貨旺
季，第四季營收有望再度衝高。整體而言，在休閒車輛與電動
機車業務雙引擎帶動下，公司下半年營收動能無虞，本業獲利
維持在高檔區域並保有顯著的年成長動能 ， 加上轉投資事業獲
利成長，研究部上修豐祥今年度獲利9%，預估豐祥2026年合併
營收94.68億元(+39%YOY)，毛利率23.4%(+1.0pct)，費用率
9.4%(+0.8pct)， 預估稅後淨利11.94億元(+41%YOY)， 預估2026
年EPS 17.17元。

3. 三大成長引擎接棒，2027年業績將再創高： 
展望2027年，研究部認為豐祥將繼續保有偏強的營運動能，主
要來自於三大核心領域 ：(1)休閒車輛新訂單持續堆疊與放量 。
特別是受惠於北美供應鏈轉移效益 ， 豐祥對客戶的供應滲透率
提升，北美Honda、Harley-Davidson等客戶陸續有新機種將於
明年量產；亦有日系客戶的新訂單將提前至明年出貨，使休閒
車業務在2027年依然是貢獻整體營收增長的最主要基石；

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)越南當地電動機車與電動汽車市場規模擴大，相關訂單持
續增加，為既有業務提供成長支撐 ；(3)電子新業務的實質貢
獻。豐祥切入伺服器機櫃供應鏈，經過2026年下半年的試產準
備後，2027年將迎來訂單放量，預期電子業務佔營收比重躍升
至5%以上。此外，休閒車輛占比提高，產品組合將繼續優化，
毛利率能再墊高；配合物流逐漸回歸自營管理，費用率亦有望
下降，將進一步提升盈利能力。研究部預估豐祥2027年合併營
收116.46 億元(+23%YOY) ，毛 利 率23.7%(+0.3pct) ，費 用 率
9.1%(-0.3pct)， 預估稅後淨利14.70億元(+23%YOY)， 預估2027
年EPS 21.09元。

### Page 1

HD현대중공업은 전일(9/10) 대출력 발전엔진 (20MW, 9MW 급) 및 SMR 캐파 
확장을 위한 신규시설투자를 공시했다. 규모는 총 1조 722억원이며, 그 중 엔진 관련 
투자는 8,336억원, SMR 관련 투자는 2,386억원이다.

엔진 관련 투자에서는 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 확장을 위한 신규 공장 신설 및 기존 
공장 생산 설비 구축을 진행하고자 한다. 동사의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파는 기존 3GW 
규모이며, 이번 증설을 통해 신규 공장 캐파 3GW, 기존 공장 증설분 1.2GW가 
더해져 2028년 5월 기준 최종적으로 7.2GW 규모가 될 것으로 예상된다. 영암 
공장과 신축 공장에서는 데이터센터향으로 납품될 9MW 및 20MW 규모의 대출력 
엔진 위주, 기존 울산 공장은 3~5MW 규모의 선박용 엔진 위주의 생산이 이루어질 
계획이다. 이번 공장 신축 완료 이후에도 가용 부지가 남아있어 시장 수요에 따라 
향후 추가적인 캐파 확대 역시 고려할 수 있다.

SMR 투자의 경우 현대건설/테라파워와의 협력을 위한 SMR 주기기 연 2기 생산 
캐파 확보를 목적으로 진행되었다. 1기 당 출력 용량은 345MW이며, 구체적인 생산 
단가 및 물량의 경우 테라파워사와 지속 협의 중으로 동사의 미래 먹거리로서 
중장기적인 성장성에 기여할 전망이다.

데이터센터향 수요 성장에 대한 확신은 실제 엔진 업체들의 수주 인콰이어리 및 신규 
투자에서 드러나고 있다. 엔진 발전 시장 상위 업체 바르질라와 에버런스가 올해 
결정한 증설 규모는 총 4.5GW로 파악되며, 동사 역시 같은 행보로 신규 시장에서 
유의미한 점유율을 확보할 것으로 기대된다. 동사의 데이터센터향 4행정 중속엔진 
수주 단가를 고려했을 때 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 가능하다. 또한, 현재 동사 
엔진사업부의 캐시카우를 담당하고 있는 2행정 저속엔진 대비 좋은 수익성을 보여줄 
것으로 기대되는 바, 데이터센터향 생산이 시작될 28년 하반기부터 점진적으로 엔진 
믹스 개선 및 마진 성장이 가속화될 것으로 전망한다.

질문 내용 
1) 준공 이후 램프업 소요 기간 28 년 5 월 준공 이후 실질적 운영은 6 월 시작 
  28 년 30%, 29 년 60% 30 년 100% 가동 목표 
  4Q28 신공장 첫 엔진 생산 가능 예상 
2) 데이터센터향 엔진 수주 인콰이어리 지속 증가 추세, 8/10 수주 이후에도 추가 증가 중 
  인콰이어리가 모두 수주로 이어지진 않지만 증설 결정에는 충분한 수요라는 판단 
3) 발전용 엔진 인콰이어리 믹스 신규 인콰이어리 기준 육상용과 FDC 향 6:4~5:5 비율 
4) 경쟁사 대비 캐파 및 라인업 비교 바르질라, 에버런스가 발전용 엔진 시장 상위기업 
  양사 합산 증설 규모 4.5GW 추정,  
  경쟁사와의 라인업은 동사와 유사, 대출력 엔진 9MW, 20MW 급 위주 
5) 공장별 혼용 생산 가능 여부 기존 울산 공장 테스트 베드는 3-5MW 급 선박용에 최적화로 선박용 생산에 집중 계획 
  영암 공장 및 신규 공장은 모든 종류 엔진 생산 가능 
6) 바르질라 AM 사업과의 수익성 비교 바르질라와 유사하게 LTSA(장기유지보수계약) 구조로 진행될 가능성 높음 
  수익성 역시 바르질라와 유사한 수준 기대

자료: HD현대중공업, 상상인증권

## Document 144: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

2026-09-17

## Document 153: AI Adoption Tracker_ August 2026_ Adoption Rises to 22.4%

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Adoption Tracker_ August 2026_ Adoption Rises to 22.4%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We update our AI adoption tracker through August 2026. n
AI-related investment growth remains strong, particularly for semiconductors, n
where equity analysts expect global revenue to reach $863bn (ann.) by the end 
of 2026. 2026 gross profit forecast revisions since 2022Q3 for AI-exposed global 
public companies currently stands at $1.1tn. AI-related hardware investment in 
the US national accounts also continues to rise and now stands at $485bn 
(3-month annualized average; 1.5% of GDP) above its 2022 level. AI-related 
hardware shipments from Taiwan have grown by 76% since 2022 and US AI 
hardware net imports ticked up to $55.9bn in June.  
AI adoption by firms rose by 0.9pp to 22.4% among US establishments (with n
adoption expected to rise to 25.9% in the next 6 months) according to the 
Census Bureau’s Business Trends and Outlook Survey (BTOS). Information is 
leading sector adoption at nearly 50%. At a detailed subsector level, computing 
and publishing firms continue to lead adoption, and warehousing and 
miscellaneous manufacturing firms report the largest expected increase in 
adoption over the next six months. Establishments with over 250 employees 
continue to lead in adoption (40%; latest six survey average) while adoption has 
grown more slowly recently among smaller firms. Other business surveys note 
that 40% of surveyed organizations have begun deploying and scaling AI agents. 
AI’s labor market impact remains visible but narrow. We continue to observe n
employment drags in specific industries where AI-use cases have been 
established such as marketing, graphic design, customer service, and some tech 
occupations. However, this is offset by construction job growth in data 
center-exposed categories, which is up 209k since 2022 (relative to broader 
construction employment trends). The tech share of total employment continues 
to decline relative to the pre-2022 trend. The correlation between AI adoption 
and broad labor market slack measures remains limited but has strengthened in 
recent months. Corporate layoffs attributed to AI affected 11k employees in 
June, bringing the 2026 YTD total to 113k. 22% of Russell 3000 companies 
mentioned AI and labor-related keywords in 2026Q2. 
We continue to observe large impacts on labor productivity in the limited areas n
where generative AI has been deployed but only modest signs of economy-wide 
impacts. Academic studies imply a 23% average uplift to productivity, while 
company anecdotes imply slightly larger efficiency gains of around 32%. 
Sarah Dong

Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC

Note: The Census Bureau has rephrased questions from asking about AI use in 
“producing goods and services” to asking about AI use in “any business functions” 
starting from the December 4, 2025 release. There was a level shift observed in 
conjunction with the new question wording, and we will be tracking adoption rates 
under the new methodology going forward.

### Page 11

Exhibit 15: 40% of Surveyed Organizations Have Begun Deploying and Scaling AI Agents 
Source Date Current Adoption / Impacts Future Adoption / Impacts
McKinsey August 2026
40% of large organization respondents have adopted AI agents and are 
scaling their deployment throughout the organization, up from 27% last year. 
Nearly a third of respondents reported that they had used agentic coding 
tools to build software in-house instead of purchasing it. 80% of respondents 
said AI has improved their individual productivity. 
60% of surveyed organizations expect to increase their AI investments in the 
year ahead. 39% of respondents expect AI-related declines in their 
organization's total employment in the coming year. 
Gallup July 2026
47% of surveyed US employees in Q2 said their organization has integrated 
AI tools to improve workplace productivity, up from 41% last quarter. 
Individual AI use at work also increased to 52% of respondents. Among AI 
users, the most common uses of AI are writing and editing (51%), search or 
research (49%), and general assistance or problem-solving (39%).
The highest productivity ratings come from employees using AI for coding 
assistance and automation or process automation. Employees who use AI 
for a greater variety of tasks were also  more likely to report that AI positively 
affects their personal productivity. Although corporate adoption and AI 
awareness has grown notably, one in five employees remain unsure if AI has 
been integrated at their organization
WRITER April 2026
52% of employees say they've used AI agents over the past year. 
Employees reported saving around 6 hours per week with AI tools, while 
executives reported saving twice as much time at nearly 12 hours per week. 
The top use cases include editing and proofreading, personalized 
communication, and data analysis and insights. Few surveyed leaders 
reported seeing significant ROI from generative AI (29%) or AI agents (23%).
69% of executives report doing layoffs due to AI, but 39% admit their 
company doesn't have a formal strategy to drive revenue from AI. 
Harvard Business 
Review and 
Appian
April 2026
Most organizations have moved beyond the experimentation phase with AI 
and 58% of surveyed organizations have AI in production. 69% of 
respondents reported that legacy systems are limiting their organization's 
ability to scale AI. Most respondents are seeing some returns from AI 
investments, but only 16% report realizing a high degree of measurable 
value. 
Many organizations are currently exploring agentic AI systems (under 
consideration by 62% of respondents and currently used by 25%). Although 
nearly all organizations agree that clear guardrails are needed for AI agent 
adoption, fewer than half say their organization has defined such rules.
Gallup April 2026
Half of all surveyed adults reported using AI in their jobs and 27% of 
employees in AI-adopting organizations say that their workplace has 
changed in disruptive ways due to AI in the past year. 65% of adopters said 
that AI has improved their productivity and efficiency, regardless of how often 
they personally use AI at work. 
Eighteen percent of all U.S. employees say it is very or somewhat likely their 
job will be eliminated within the next five years due to AI or automation. 
Among employees working in organizations that have adopted AI, that share 
rises to 23%. However, employees in AI-adopting organizations were more 
likely to report both current expansions and reductions in headcount. 
NVIDIA March 2026
64% of respondents said their organizations are actively using AI in their 
operations. 28% said they're still in the assessment phase, while 8% said 
they're not using AI and have no plans to. Regional adoption among 
respondents was 70% in North America, 65% in EMEA, and 63% in APAC. 
Larger companies (with over 1000 employees) demonstrated broader 
adoption, deployed more use cases, and reported greater ROI. More than 
half of respondents (53%) said improved employee productivity was one of 
the biggest impacts AI had on business operations.
86% of respondents said their AI budgets would increase this year and 
nearly 40% said budgets would increase by 10% or more. Respondents 
reported that workflow and operations and retaining the right expertise to 
scale AI solutions were the key challenges to adoption. 
Deloitte January 2026
In the latest survey, 34% of companies were starting to use AI to deeply 
transform their businesses, 30% were designing key processes around AI, 
and the remaining 37% were using AI at the surface level with little or no 
change to underlying business processes. 84% of companies have not 
redesignated jobs or work tasks around AI capabilities. 25% of surveyed 
leaders reported that AI is having a transformative effect on their 
organizations, more than double from 12% a year ago. 
Survey results showed that insufficient employee skills were the largest 
barrier to integrating AI into existing workflows. Nearly three-quarters of 
surveyed companies expect to be using agentic AI at least moderately in two 
years. 
Gallup December 2025
The percentage of US employees who reported using AI at work at least a 
few times a week increased from 19% in 2025Q2 to 23% in Q3. 
Unsurprisingly, employees working in knowledge-based jobs (such as tech 
or professional services) were more likely to use AI than those in frontline 
positions. Most common use cases include using AI to consolidate 
information or data, idea generation, and automating basic tasks. Users 
most often use AI chatbots and virtual assistants. 
Gallup research has shown that employee AI adoption is strongly associated 
with having greater managerial support for AI and strategic integration of AI 
in their role, indicating that a strategic people-first approach is needed for 
effective AI adoption.

## Document 157: AI双视角：行业捕获率与价值创造率

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI双视角：行业捕获率与价值创造率.pdf
Report source: 国金证券
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 1

基本内容： 
在此前的《AI洪流三部曲》中，我们尝试给出了AI技术所影响的劳动力规模以及对应的薪资水平；但在理解潜在收
入上限时依然缺少关键一环，即 AI 技术的增效水平，进而量化潜在可代替的支出水平。除此之外，行业视角下捕获
率的高低也越发关键，尤其是当AI收入焦点从单一的Coding转向科研（AI4S） 、办公场景、文娱传媒等更广泛领域。

AI技术捕获率与价值创造率实际上是站在不同视角看待技术发展： 对于模型商而言， 捕获率决定着未来的潜在收入水
平；对于使用AI的企业而言，价值创造率决定着AI在纯替代视角下能享受的降本增效水平。前者对应着短期的市场
景气度和硬件侧的交易空间，而后者则对应着更长期的数据中心回报率和模型收入能力。

不同行业的捕获率差异巨大，计算机行业不到10%，金融行业不到2%；AI平均增效约7 倍，计算机行业享受约四分之
一的“价值创造”。

在数据允许的情况下，未来有三个非常重要的观察视角。

首先是价值创造水平，因为当前AI产业自证需要的是可量化的收入，但是大量的福利通过消费者剩余的方式被AI使
用者所享受，如何定量这些水平，而非简单盯住ARR和硬件订单，会成为从硬件交易向软件/应用过渡的重要参考。

其次是薪资水平的参考尺度，尽管在短期尺度 AI 在大多数行业依然是替代型技术，但是现代社会的多任务属性决定
着劳动力薪资或许并不是当前AI收入的主要来源，换言之对于AI的劳动力冲击可以相对乐观一些。

最后是研究方法的缺乏，如果AI 真的用以创造，那 BLS 视角下的 755 个职业，以及背后对应的数千个任务属性是否
能够准确描述 AI 在当前社会正在做什么？如果越来越多的任务被划归为“无法定义” ，如果AI 成为一个多模态，多
任务并行处理的工具，我们讨论的就不仅仅是某个职业或者某个行业。

金融、计算机、文娱传媒与科学研究完全可以在某个时间被 AI 所融合，这或许才是未来软件应用所追求的方向：上
限不是单一行业的单一薪资，而是全社会的总价值创造能力。

风险提示 
1）宏观经济数据频率较低，对于跨境行业数据估计存在滞后性；2）美国产业政策受党派影响明显，执政党派的更换
可能引发产业政策回溯；3）AI技术发展与产业投资存在较大不确定性。

### Page 2

宏观经济点评

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 2

扫码获取更多服务

### Page 3

宏观经济点评

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明 3

扫码获取更多服务 
在此前的《AI洪流三部曲》中，我们尝试给出了AI技术所影响的劳动力规模以及对应的薪资水平；但在理解潜在收
入上限时依然缺少关键一环，即 AI 技术的增效水平，进而量化潜在可代替的支出水平。除此之外，行业视角下捕获
率的高低也越发关键，尤其是当AI收入焦点从单一的Coding转向科研（AI4S）、办公场景、文娱传媒等更广泛领域。 
AI技术捕获率与价值创造率实际上是站在不同视角看待技术发展： 对于模型商而言， 捕获率决定着未来的潜在收入水
平；对于使用AI的企业而言，价值创造率决定着AI在纯替代视角下能享受的降本增效水平。前者对应着短期的市场
景气度和硬件侧的交易空间，而后者则对应着更长期的数据中心回报率和模型收入能力。 
尤其是最近的 Muse 应用可能采用“抽成”的商业模式（类似瓦特改良蒸汽机的收入来自于煤炭节约量的三分之一），
可以理解为在当前这个Agent模态下进行技术改良，这也更加说明理解“AI效率提升”的重要性。 
我们根据 Anthropic 的公开数据，重新构造了美国全量职业视角下的AI 维度，补充了职业/行业视角下 AI 的增效倍
率、AI技术所创造的价值分布以及不同行业的捕获率水平——这三个关键计算结果。由于数据有限，以及统计口径的
原因，在部分指标估算中存在较大偏离，在第三部分我们将进行充分讨论。 
一、不同行业的捕获率差异巨大，计算机行业不到10%，金融行业不到 2% 
计算即时捕获率，以及不同行业潜在空间的计算方法并不复杂：只需要用ARR收入乘不同行业使用率作为行业实际收
入贡献，将结果与实际暴露度下对应的薪资总数对比，即可得到行业视角下捕获率的近似描述。 
然而，该方法的难点在于Token消耗量的行业分布数据（以及对应价格）难以挖掘；我们只能找到Anthropic公布的
“以任务次数调用”统计的使用率数据。这意味着一次计算机行业任务与餐饮服务业任务调用含有相等权重，但现实
情况是计算机行业单次任务消耗的 Token或是其他行业的数十倍。由于这种有偏对应(只能用“调用次数”而不是“消
耗token数”来代表AI收入)，导致部分行业（例如金融业）的捕获率可能偏低。 
首先，我们“再次”展示截止到2026年3 月（最后的数据更新月份），行业视角下的实际技术暴露度与理论技术暴露
度水平。理论上最有可能被AI替代的行业是计算机与数学(87.6%）、商业与金融（78.2%）以及法律(78.0%），但实际
观察到的暴露度排序与理论值并不一致， 实际暴露度最高的行业是计算机与数学(35.3%）、办公室与行政支持 （33.2%）
以及销售相关职位(24.6%）。 
对于这个暴露度数值，我们再次强调这并不能理解为有35%的员工已经失业，或者有 35%的任务已经可以 AI 自动化，
而应该理解为“将每一个职业拆解成不同的任务后，以完成每个任务的时间加权的情况下，有 35%的任务已经在
Anthropic的AI使用中被观察到” 。

接着，我们展示以任务次数为权重的统计方式下，美国不同行业的AI使用率占比（包括API调用和订阅端） ：计算机
行业占据了约25%的使用次数（尽管仍被严重低估） ，文体传媒业接近15%，而金融行业仅占5%——单次任务越复杂，
图1：不同行业的理论暴露度与实际暴露度

来源：Anthropic，BLS，OpenAI，国金证券研究所

扫码获取更多服务 
环节越多，调用的Token也会越多，而金融业采用AI的任务更多以“大篇幅”为主，占比自然较低。

基于以上数据，我们对于捕获率水平进行直接的计算：因为数据本身只更新至5 月，我们假设 AI 大模型的总 ARR 收
入在 800 亿美元水平，得到了捕获率的结果如下：计算机行业的捕获率大约在9%，而金融行业的捕获率仅在 1.9%。
我们可以从数学计算的角度找到一个“解释” （但并非完全合理） ：金融行业的分子过小，即使用率水平低；但分母过
大，24.5%的实际暴露度水平对应着约2500亿美元的总薪资水平。

扫码获取更多服务

最后，我们也可以对未来潜在捕获收入进行一个估计，即用当前不同行业的捕捉水平去对标未来理论暴露薪资收入。
在这个纯替代视角下，潜在捕获收入在4000 亿美元水平，其中计算机行业贡献500 亿美元，建筑工程和文体传媒位
居第二和第三位，分别为410 和 360 亿美元。虽然金融行业贡献的 AI 收入被低估，但就整体数值和行业排序来看，
基本也符合产业发展逻辑。 
一方面，4000亿美元基本是替代视角下ARR收入的某种边界共识。另一方面，除了计算机外，建筑工程（辅助设计）
与运输搬运（自动驾驶、运力规划）的收入贡献排名靠前，与 AI 的流程优化有关；而文体传媒与教培行业则体现出
了AI的“定制能力” 。 
图4：总潜在捕获收入约 4000亿美元

来源：Anthropic Economic Index，国金证券研究所 
二、AI 平均增效约 7 倍，计算机行业享受约四分之一的“价值创造” 
接下来，我们换到AI使用者的视角，讨论对于那些使用AI的企业/职业/任务，技术本身带来了多大的效率提升。如
果Meta公布的Muse真的要采用抽成而非订阅模式，这会成为未来理解Agent发展的新关键。这里我们从“消费者剩
余”的角度来理解接下来要展示的数据——当使用者付给模型商的水平恒定之后，AI创造的额外价值即为 AI技术带
给消费者的“消费者剩余” 。 
根据 Anthropic公布的全量数据， 我们得到了美国755个职业 （部分空缺） 对应的每次使用AI完成任务的平均时间，
同时 Anthropic也公布了反事实估计值，即如果这个任务由人类自主完成，所需要的平均时间。基于这两个数据，我
们可以得到初步的AI增效倍率，再根据每一个职业的使用占比和对应的时薪水平，我们可以得到应用AI技术后，行
业视角下的价值创造分布情况。 
我们通过举例方式进行简要说明：电脑程序员(行业名称：Computer Programmer，职业编号：15-1251)，使用AI情
况下平均任务时间为52.35分钟，不使用AI 完成时间（估计值）为9.12小时，即547.2分钟——AI的增效倍率则为
547.2/52.35 = 10.45。在对每一个职业进行类似统计后， 以使用占比作为权重加权可以得到行业视角下的增效倍率。 
自下而上的计算整合后，我们观察到使用AI技术平均增效7 倍，增效最明显的是行政支持行业，约10.3倍，最低的
是社会服务和医疗，仅2.8倍。计算机行业大约在7.2倍，而金融行业在22个行业增效倍率排名第二，高达8.7 倍。

从职业视角来看（排除使用率为 0 的职业），AI带来的效率提升最明显的职业是微生物学家(20.96 倍)，前20当中包
括了前沿科学领域，行政支持领域以及艺术行业。这也比较符合当前对AI发展方向的期待，科研是AI技术一个新的
扩展领域，尤其是对于大量可重复，有方法论的研究来说，AI的降本增效更加明显——近期数学界千禧年问题的持续
突破，以及数百个前沿问题的集中解决是一个很好的例证。

三、AI 的价值创造与“消费者剩余” 
有了每个职业的增效水平，以及 AI 使用率占比后，我们可以计算职业/行业视角下的 AI 价值创造分布。依然以计算
机程序员为例进行说明，已知其使用 AI 情况下平均任务时间为 52.35 分钟，不使用 AI 完成时间（估计值）为 9.12
小时，即547.2分钟，因此使用AI节约了494.85分钟（8.25小时） ，则可以粗略的理解为AI单次任务的价值创造为
8.25小时乘以时薪。 
问题在于，我们只知道使用率分布，不知道具体调用次数，因此无法知道每一个职业/行业具体的价值创造水平，但

扫码获取更多服务 
可以算出价值创造的分布——即每个行业分了“多少比例”的 AI价值创造蛋糕，但是不知道这个蛋糕“有多大”。 
单一职业的价值创造比例为 Share𝑖，其中N 作为总次数会被约分，使用率、时间以及每个职业对应的时薪此前已举例
说明： 
𝑉𝑖 = 𝑁 × UsageRate𝑖 × (HumanTime𝑖 − AITime𝑖) × HourlyWage𝑖 
Share𝑖 = 𝑉𝑖∑ 𝑉𝑗𝐼𝑗=1  
经过计算，计算机行业获得了 AI 价值创造（消费者剩余）的 25%，文体传媒行业也获得了 AI 价值创造的近 15%，管
理、教育培训也都是使用AI的主要受益者。AI价值创造的行业排序也符合AI交易的热点方向：比如Coding的场景
应用之后， 近期火热的AI短剧行业体现了AI在文体传媒行业中的价值创造， 培训行业 （包括教培、人力资源培训等）
也可以通过AI进行更加个性化/定制化的服务。 
图7：计算机行业享受约四分之一的“价值创造”

来源：Anthropic Economic Index，BLS，国金证券研究所 
有了以上数据后，我们可以把行业的使用率与价值创造率进行对比，进而勾勒出AI使用的“性价比水平”，即是否价
值创造率高的行业使用较多。 
总的来说，两者基本匹配，这意味着AI价值创造倍率高的行业也基本对应更多的AI技术使用。但其中出现了两个相
对例外，一个是管理行业，用约 5.9%的任务次数创造了约 13.6%的“消费者剩余”，这主要依赖该行业的高附加值属
性；另一个是销售行业，用了约9.3%的任务次数，只创造了4.4%的额外价值。 
当然， 我们强调这些估计都是以“消费者剩余”的视角， 最终这些价值以什么样方式转化（是增加劳动者的休闲时间，
还是增加资本家的投资回报） ，依然存在较大不确定性。

扫码获取更多服务 
图8：从价值创造/使用率的角度，各行业基本匹配

来源：Anthropic Economic Index，BLS，国金证券研究所 
我们再次强调的是，因为缺少总调用次数，无法直接计算这些消费者剩余的绝对规模，这是个较为遗憾的地方。 
这里我们引用一些前沿论文的估算：Brynjolfsson等人的2026年4 月论文测算(What is Generative AI Worth)：
站在 2025 年末的节点，全球生成式 AI 的收入约 142 亿美元，对应的美国消费者剩余约 1162 亿美元，即倍率约为
1162/142 = 8.2倍（假设绝大多数都发生在美国） ；这与Anthropic估算的约7 倍的AI技术平均增效水平较为接近。 
当然两者的视角并不一致，Brynjolfsson等人的论文主要是基于AI收入，而我们则通过节约时间与薪资倒推；但大
致的消费者剩余规模可能就在5-10倍的量级水平。 
总的来说，目前AI创造的大部分消费福利仍由用户而非厂商获得，AI已经创造了很大的隐性价值，但产业收入和利
润对这些价值的捕获仍明显滞后——但是如果 AI 不可避免地从硬件交易转向关注软件，这个“价格创造倍率（消费
者剩余） ”视角将更加重要。 
四、以上估算所存在的弊端，以及未来观察视角 
我们最后单独讨论捕获率视角与价值创造视角估算所存在的弊端， 这并不只因为某些估计中呈现出了极大的方差水平，
更是希望引发对未来观察AI收入水平尺度的一些思考。 
正如前文所反复强调的那样，我们的核心估计主要依赖于任务次数的分布，而非Token消耗量，天然低估了一些高收
入创造能力行业（比如计算机、金融等）的占比，进而低估了AI技术的潜在捕获率。 
还有对捕获率的恒定假设（外推到理论总薪资），也限制了替代型AI技术的潜在收入空间——因为随着模型能力的快
速增长，以及技术自然渗透的S曲线，捕获率会随着时间推移而逐渐上升。 
再深一层的思考，薪资是衡量替代型AI技术潜在收入的度量尺度，但创造型AI技术已经出现，同时过去普遍担忧的
广泛裁员似乎并未发生，那么是否有衡量AI未来收入的更好尺度？这个问题需要思考。 
其次是技术暴露度的度量意义或被高估，Agent进入到第四个季度，尽管美国信息技术业的裁员依然在缓慢推进，但
总的来说我们并没有看到预想中的劳动力冲击。根本问题是观察到任务被暴露，并不等于 AI 能自主完全执行任务，
因此暴露度高估了工资池范围（实际薪资暴露），进而低估了捕获率水平（薪资作为分母被高估） 。 
另一个高估技术暴露度的因素来自于当前更多职业都具备多任务属性(multi-task)； 当前社会的主要工作岗位都对应
着多个任务，因此在职业主任务中AI 带来的效率提升/技术暴露并不能完全等效于整个职业对AI 技术的反馈。且大
多数岗位都存在多任务串联的特性，即任务链条存在前置条件；这些都意味着从技术到效率再到就业与收入的影响并
非按比例传导，而是有所损耗。

宏观经济点评

扫码获取更多服务 
图9：AI 场景中所观察到的多任务情景比例

来源：Anthropic Economic Index，BLS，国金证券研究所 
我们理解，在数据允许的情况下，未来有三个非常重要的观察视角。 
首先是价值创造水平，因为当前AI产业自证需要的是可量化的收入，但是大量的福利通过消费者剩余的方式被AI使
用者所享受，如何定量这些水平，而非简单盯住ARR和硬件订单，会成为从硬件交易向软件/应用过渡的重要参考。 
其次是薪资水平的参考尺度，尽管在短期尺度 AI 在大多数行业依然是替代型技术，但是现代社会的多任务属性决定
着劳动力薪资或许并不是当前AI收入的主要来源，换言之对于AI的劳动力冲击可以相对乐观一些。 
最后是研究方法的缺乏，如果AI 真的用以创造，那 BLS 视角下的 755 个职业，以及背后对应的数千个任务属性是否
能够准确描述 AI 在当前社会正在做什么？如果越来越多的任务被划归为“无法定义” ，如果AI 成为一个多模态，多
任务并行处理的工具，我们讨论的就不仅仅是某个职业或者某个行业。金融、计算机、文娱传媒与科学研究完全可以
在某个时间被 AI 所融合，这或许才是未来软件应用所追求的方向：上限不是单一行业的单一薪资，而是全社会的总
价值创造能力。 
风险提示 
1）AI技术数据更新频率较低，无法及时反馈模型能力的大幅提升；2）美国产业政策受党派影响明显，执政党派的更
换可能引发产业政策回溯；3）AI技术发展与产业投资存在较大不确定性。

## Document 160: AI模型遭遇消费降级：顶级模型使用占比从55%降至14%，用户转向性价比

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI模型遭遇消费降级：顶级模型使用占比从55%降至14%，用户转向性价比.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI ⼤模型竞争正从追求极致性能转向注重性价⽐。 OpenRouter 数据显⽰，前⼗款⾼性能模
型的使⽤占⽐从 2025 年⼀季度的 55% 降⾄ 2026 年三季度的 14% 。 Meta 此时推出 Muse
Wonder ，试图证明⾃⾝的前沿模型实⼒，但性能领先能否转化为实际⽤⼾需求，仍有待市
场检验。
摘要：
AI ⼤模型⾏业正⾯临⼀个反差：科技巨头仍在竞相推出性能更强的前沿模型，⽤⼾却开始转向更
便宜、⾜以满⾜实际需求的产品。 Meta 此时推出 Muse Wonder ，试图证明⾃⾝的前沿模型实
⼒，但市场需求正从追求极致性能转向注重性价⽐。
据 The Information 近⽇报道，通过 OpenRouter 调⽤的 AI 模型中，使⽤量排名前⼗的⾼性能模型
占⽐已从 2025 年第⼀季度的 55% 降⾄ 2026 年第三季度的 14% 。随着低成本模型的能⼒不断提
升，越来越多⽤⼾发现，完成⽇常任务并不需要调⽤最昂贵的模型。
这意味着， AI ⾏业的竞争正在从单纯⽐拼模型性能，转向⽐拼性能、成本与实际应⽤价值。对于

Meta ⽽⾔，即使 Muse Wonder 能够在特定任务上达到领先⽔平，也仍需证明这种性能优势能够
转化为⽤⼾需求。
“ 前沿 ” 标签泛滥，模型性能竞赛仍在升温
近期，主要 AI 公司密集推出旗舰模型，并强调其技术领先性。⾕歌推出 Gemini 4 Argon ，称其在
多个领域的复杂⼯作流中达到前沿⽔平； OpenAI 发布 GPT-6 Astra ，宣称其在计算机操作、软件
⼯程、⽹络安全及专业⼯作等领域达到最先进⽔平； Anthropic 则推出 Claude Fable 5.1 和
Mythos 5.1 ，将其定位为全球领先的编程与知识⼯作模型。
Meta 推出 Muse Wonder ，也是在这⼀背景下重新强调⾃⾝的技术竞争⼒。经历约 14 个⽉的⼤规
模招聘扩张后，公司希望借助新模型证明⾃⼰仍处于 AI 前沿。不过，当各家⼚商都在强调性能
领先时， “ 前沿 ” 本⾝越来越难以成为差异化优势。
据报道， Meta 计划将 Muse Wonder ⽤于⽀持其个⼈智能体产品 Muse 。但新模型的技术表现能否

转化为⽤⼾可感知的体验提升，以及这种提升是否⾜以⽀撑更⾼的使⽤成本，仍有待验证。
低成本模型崛起，⽤⼾更看重性价⽐
OpenRouter 的数据揭⽰了需求端的变化：⾼性能模型的使⽤占⽐⼤幅下降，反映出⽤⼾正在重
新权衡模型能⼒与调⽤成本。随着低成本模型不断进步，许多⽇常任务已不再需要最昂贵的模
型。
企业尤其倾向于根据任务难度分配模型：常规⼯作交给价格更低的模型，只有复杂推理、专业
分析等⾼要求任务才调⽤顶级模型。这种做法既能满⾜实际需求，也能控制 AI ⽀出。
消费者市场也⾯临类似变化。对普通⽤⼾⽽⾔，模型能否准确回答问题、顺利完成任务，往往
⽐其是否达到⾏业性能榜单的顶端更重要。当 “ ⾜够好⽤ ” 的模型能够以更低价格满⾜⼤部分需求
时，顶级模型即使持续刷新性能纪录，也未必能获得相应的使⽤增⻓。
对 Meta 等科技巨头⽽⾔，挑战已不只是推出更强的模型，还在于证明性能提升能够带来真实的
⽤⼾需求。在 AI 竞争从追逐性能转向衡量性价⽐的过程中，技术领先与商业成功之间的关系正在
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year

• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

.

Quarterly

fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies
Banque de France
Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.

## Document 230: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 12-16

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 12-16.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16
Date:    9 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 12-16
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 11 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week was relatively quiet on the economic data front, but the
indicators released pointed to continued economic strength. ADP weekly
payroll gains increased for the fifth consecutive week, reaching a three-
month high, while jobless claims fell to multi-year lows. The ISM NM-PMI
remained solid at 54.9 in September, though its prices paid component
jumped to 74.0, the highest since July 2022. Redbook same-store sales
rose 8.3% y/y during the week of October 2, signaling continued strength

in consumer spending, while the New York Fed's consumer survey
showed expected spending growth and one-year-ahead inflation
expectations both reaching three-year highs.
The data suggest that the Fed has good reason to be hawkish.
Accordingly, September's FOMC meeting minutes showed that most
policymakers favored another rate hike by year-end, with several judging
monetary policy to be barely restrictive, if at all. We agree that further
tightening is warranted. However, recent comments by New York Fed

President John Williams, Fed Vice Chair Philip Jefferson, and Governor
Christopher Waller suggest flexibility on the timing. The markets assign a
17.2% probability to an October hike and a 71.6% probability of a
December hike.
This week's inflation reports could change those odds. A hotter-than-
expected CPI reading on Wednesday could prompt the markets to price in
an October hike, pressuring the Fed after September's unanimous 25bps
increase, which Fed Chair Kevin Warsh described as removing "a dose of

accommodation." An in-line or cooler CPI reading would support patience.
Warsh speaks Thursday evening at the IMF and World Bank annual
meetings in Bangkok.
Here's more:

### Page 4

(1) Inflation. The September CPI and PPI reports will be released on
Wednesday and Thursday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation
Nowcast model projects a hot 0.53% m/m increase in headline CPI, while
core CPI is expected to rise a more modest 0.2%. That would put headline
and core CPI inflation at 3.6% and 2.4% y/y, respectively. September is
expected to mark the 67th consecutive month of CPI inflation exceeding
2.0% y/y, underscoring the persistence of inflationary pressures (chart).

Adding to inflation concerns, the averages of the prices-paid and prices-
received indexes of five regional Fed banks’ business surveys rose to their
highest levels since 2022 in September (chart). Both have historically
tracked headline CPI inflation closely.

### Page 5

The same indicators have also closely tracked PPI final demand inflation,
which was already running hot at 5.4% y/y in August (chart).
(2) Monthly Treasury statement. The federal budget deficit totaled $1.77
trillion over the 12 months through August, while outstanding marketable
Treasury securities increased by $2.12 trillion over the 12 months through
September (chart). The recent slowdown in Treasury borrowing suggests
that the deficit may have narrowed somewhat. Tuesday's report will reveal

the final budget deficit for fiscal 2026, which ended on September 30.
(3) Consumer spending. Thursday's September retail sales report will
provide the first official reading on consumer spending for the month. We
expect another solid increase, as Redbook same-store sales rose 8.3%
y/y in September, well above the 2025 average of 5.8%. Redbook sales

### Page 6

historically have tracked official retail sales closely, suggesting that
consumer spending maintained strong momentum through the end of Q3
(chart).
(4) Manufacturing. The week will also provide important insights into the
manufacturing sector, with the October New York and Philadelphia Fed
banks’ business surveys due Thursday, followed by September's industrial
production and capacity utilization reports on Friday.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours rose to 84.4 in September, the

highest since August 2023, continuing their recent upward trend (chart).
Immediate expensing under the OBBBA, reshoring, and the AI buildout
are helping to reinvigorate manufacturing activity. With skilled labor in
short supply, manufacturers appear to be meeting stronger demand partly
by increasing the hours worked by existing employees. The rise in
aggregate hours worked suggests that manufacturing production
expanded at a healthy pace in September.

### Page 7

The New York and Philadelphia Fed manufacturing surveys provide
another reason for optimism. Their average manufacturing PMI rose to
22.7 in September, the highest since 2021 (chart). Historically, the
average has closely tracked manufacturing production growth, suggesting
that factory output could accelerate further in the months ahead.
💡
Join the discussion with Ed below! To leave comments or questions,
log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end

of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

## Document 264: GFHK - UMC update

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - UMC update.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meaningful Re‑Rating Path into 2H26–2028

Maintained  Buy 
 Target price NT$215

Raise TP to NT$215: UMC’s share price underperformed Taiex in July/Aug but rebounded on 
rising expectations of price hikes and optical opportunities ( our report : PIC/TFLN Upside  
Unlocks a Higher -Margin Cycle  on Aug 24 ). We continue to expect UMC to be re ‑rated, 
supported by profitability optimization through 2H26–2027 (higher UTR, price hikes, improving 
mix, etc), followed by a shift to a new growth phase from 2028 driven by FinFET 12nm and an 
“optical full‑solution model”. Reflecting stronger pricing and margins, we lift 2026E/2027E EPS 
by +2%/+8% and raise our target multiple to 25x 2027E EPS on a stronger margin/growth 
outlook, resulting in a new TP of NT$215. Our model indicates UMC should maintain solid 
FCF and dividend yields. 
Earnings optimization in 2H26–2027 amid transfer‑order gains: We see UMC is in a phase 
of profitability optimization supported by rising UTR, pricing, and improving mix. We expect 
UTR for: 1) 12” to reach ~93% by end ‑2026 on strong DDIC volumes from Novatek and 
Samsung LSI at 22/28nm and Apple’s Mac  at 55/65nm, with upside from Sony’s sensors 
(>10KPM at 22/28nm); 2) 8” to ~90% by end ‑2026 and full loading by 2H27  on intact 
demand (datacenter PMIC, MCU, embedded memory, etc), Samsung LSI’s scale ‑down in 8” 
driving PMIC order transfer (~70KPM in 2027, ~110KPM in 2028), and additional transfer from 
Novatek (HV) and MTK (BCD), with upside from Qualcomm PMIC in 2027. Meanwhile, pricing 
momentum extends from 2H26 (selective customers) into 1H27 (10 –30% across 12” and 8”) 
and 2H27 (ongoing negotiations), suggesting visibility extending into 2H27.  
Transformation from 2028 to “asset‑light FinFET” and “optical full solution”: We expect 
UMC’s next growth phase, likely beginning 2028, to be driven by: 1) Intel 12nm as a major 
inflection—an asset‑light FinFET model with revenue contribution from 2028, plus upside from 
further 12nm expansion tied to Apple’s DDIC migration amid “Made in U.S.”; 2) SiPh/TFLN 
scaling toward 2.4T/3.2T, with TFLN as the largest long ‑term opportunity. We estimate EPS 
contribution of NT$0.7/NT$1.6 in 2027E/2028E, supported by higher ASPs of ~$4.5k and 
wafer volumes of 20KPM/40KPM by end‑2027/2028; 3) interposer and advanced packaging 
beginning to contribute from 2H27 (interposer), supported by emerging vectors such as silicon 
capacitor/DTC, hybrid bonding, and controller ‑to‑bonding integration, positioning UMC to 
evolve beyond PIC foundry toward a broader optical integration model. 
Risks: 1) Geo-political risk; 2) Demand uncertainties; 3) Competition; 4) New product delay.

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Valuation and Recommendation  
Buy with TP of NT$215 
We rate UMC (2303 TT, UMC US) Buy and set our TP at NT$ 215, which is based on 25x our 2027E 
EPS estimate  from previous 20x due to enhanced margin and growth outlook. We adopt P/E 
methodology as we believe the market will be mainly focused on the company’s earnings outlook, and 
its earnings volatility has reduced since 2021 due to a much more diversified application mix and 
specialty position. We adopt the 2027 P/E valuation is due to the improving pricing environment in the 
longer term. Overall, we believe the  mature node industry outlook has become more favorable , and 
UMC’s mgmt. also guided a favorable pricing environment in 2026/2027. 
UMC P/E traded at an average of 1 5x since 2021, when the market accepted the P/E valuation.  Our 
target price is higher than the mid-cycle level, as we believe the rerating  catalysts are emerging amid 
favorable industry supply/demand. Meanwhile, the company continued to gain AI related business (i.e., 
networking, power, silicon photonics, advanced packaging) . We expect its ROE to reach mid -20s in 
2026E-2028E, vs. 11-12% in 2024-2025.

## Document 273: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• Indonesia's macro narrative has 
turned more constructive in 
2H26. 
• Key domestic policy concerns 
have eased. 
• Policy alignment has improved. 
• Greater fiscal discipline should 
allow BI to remain focused on 
price stability.  
• The current account is likely to 
widen as the trade surplus 
narrows. 
• Portfolio and FDI inflows should, 
however, narrow the BOP 
shortfall. 
• Few monitorables remain, 
including factors in the 2027 
fiscal math. 
• IDR performance will remain 
heavily influenced by global 
conditions.

Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.

### Page 4

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Next, the direction of travel on the 
contribution of SOE dividends from the 
investment agency to the state coffers 
for 2027 remains unclear, after being 
accounted for this year. Lastly, 
successive stimulus measures in-kind 
and wage subsidies were announced this 
year and could marginally add to the 
strain in 2027 (0.1-0.2% of GDP).

Discussions around the State Finance 
Law also warrant a watch. We maintain 
our deficit forecast at a wider -2.8% to -
2.9% range to account for any revenue 
shortfall or a gradual pace of growth. 
Nonetheless, deficits/debt are likely to 
stay below key thresholds  and not 
emerge as a flashpoint for the markets.

MONETARY POLICY ON AN EVEN 
KEEL

Bank Indonesia has left the benchmark 
rate unchanged  since July 2026, after 
100bp hikes in 1H26. While tighter global 
financial conditions and geopolitical 
risks were key risks on the horizon, the BI 
held the domestic GDP forecast at 4.9 -
5.7% (DBSf: 5.3%) alongside 
manageable inflation, notwithstanding 
El Nino-related risks. Inflation rose to 3.3% 
yoy in Sep26, towards the high end of the 
target range. Authorities have, however, 
assured that the impact of higher food 
prices due to poor weather will be 
addressed through administrative 
measures by regional bodies.

Contained inflation expectations ha ve 
reduced the urgency for BI to tighten 
policy further, allowing policymakers to 
prioritise growth and financial stability 
considerations. The emphasis on 
strengthening the balance of payments 
position reflects continued caution over 
external financing conditions and capital 
flows. However, with the rupiah 
expected to remain stable on the back of 
policy measures and fundamenta ls, the 
BI did not view current external 
pressures as severe enough to warrant 
additional tightening.

BI introduced a host of measures at the 
June to September policy meetings , 
including FX monetary hedging 
instruments and a mechanism to provide 
foreign investors with greater flexibility 
in managing IDR-denominated 
investments in Indonesia.  
These included –  
a) swap hedging per tenor bucket was 
increased to 3M - 15%; 6M - 20%; 12M - 
25%, from 12.5% earlier.  
b) DNDF incentive was increased to 6M - 
25% and 12M – 30%. An increase in FX/ 
NDF swap premium incentives for longer 
tenors will lower hedging costs and

### Page 5

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

encourage longer tenor positions, in turn 
increasing longevity of these flows;  
c) Launch of the VASTRA scheme (FX 
Market Transactions for Hedging 
through Partner Banks) got underway, 
with the naming of 14 domestic partner 
banks. This facility is designed to help 
investors transact with lower underlying 
documentation requirements, tap into 
longer banking hours , and access 
onshore hedging facilities.  
d) deepen FX and money markets by 
expanding Local Currency Transactions 
(LCT) between IDR and CNY , besides a 
few other currencies.

Policymakers are likely to tap a 
combination of administrative and 
macroprudential measures rather than 
solely interest rates to defend the 
currency as well as keep inflationary 
expectations in check.

We expect the pause on rates to extend 
into 4Q26.  This call might be at risk if 
depreciation pressures on the rupiah 
mount again on unfavourable global 
developments and if the US Fed 
undertakes a more aggressive hik ing 
cycle than our baseline (of 75bp hikes).

WIDER CAD WILL BE PARTLY 
OFFSET BY POSITIVE FLOWS

Indonesia’s 1H26 current account 
deficit (CAD) widened significantly  to -
$16bn vs -$3bn in the comparable period 
a year ago. Much of this deterioration 
was led by a narrower goods trade 
balance, while  the services deficit 
widened on the seasonal travel account 
due to the Hajj period. Higher coupon 
payments weighed on the primary 
account.  This pressure is likely to persist 
in 2H as the oil & gas trade deficit 
remains wide and export performance 
has been fi rm but tracking lower than 
regional peers, likely also impacted by 
the El Nino phenomenon in key agri 
commodities. We expect full year CAD to 
be closer to 1.8-2% of GDP from -0.1% in 
2025.

Much rests on the capital flows account. 
While 1H balance of payments (BOP) also 
widened, there was palpable 
improvement in 2Q26 vs 1Q.  2Q BOP 
narrowed significantly to $0.9bn from a 
wide $9.1bn in 1Q, helped by positive 
capital account flows – primarily 
stronger portfolio investment on SRBI 
and debt inflows, and net FDI position. 
This dependency is likely to extend to 2H, 
with capital fl ows till Sep26 holding up 
on account of hedging discounts and 
moves to improve inflows. Global 
conditions, including the narrow ID -US 
yields, however, warrant attention. 
Nonetheless, full-year BOP could remain

### Page 6

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

wide, on a scale comparable to the 2025 
gap.

IN SUMMARY

IDR markets have been relatively 
steady in October despite higher oil 
prices and a bid dollar , though 
sentiment remains sensitive to external 
developments and swings in global risk 
appetite. USDIDR traded within 17600 -
18000, while 10Y yield hovered around 
7.15-7.25%. A jump in the Aug goods 
trade surplus aided this respite, 
alongside foreign purchases of domestic 
debt, which have remained positive this 
month and on a  YTD basis. Insurers, the 
central bank, and retail investors have 
remained key anchors for the bond 
market. SRBI rates are off highs to sub -
7% levels but are attracting steady 
demand, likely encouraged by hedging 
discounts. An improved outlook for flows 
has helped contain spillovers from a 
wider current account deficit. This does 
not take away from the fact that the 
rupiah is amongst the worst performers 
on a year-to-date basis.

Separately, markets will also monitor 
developments in the political economy, 
institutional architecture, and formation, 
as well as the role of state -led agencies 
(e.g., DSI) and progress on the 
centralisation of decision -making. We 
expect the BI to keep on hold, however, 
pressure to tighten policy might return if 
the US Fed hikes rates well into 2027 
and the US dollar benefits from a risk-off 
environment, weighing on the currency.

## Document 344: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 151

New Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less 
transparent era characterized by shorter post-meeting 
statements, less forward guidance, and a more limited role for 
projections, marking a meaningful break from the 
transparency revolution that has defined central banking over 
the last quarter century. So, what could a less transparent Fed 
mean for markets and the economy? 
For answers, we turn to former Fed governors Donald Kohn, 
now Robert V. Roosa Chair in International Economics and 
senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, and Stephen Miran, 
now senior strategist at Hudson Bay Capital Management, as 
well as Jan Hatzius, GS’ Chief Economist and Head of Global 
Investment Research. 
We first ask whether scaling back Fed communication is a 
positive or negative development. Hatzius says it’s likely 
negative, though he draws a clear line between transparency 
and forward guidance. He argues that clarity about the Fed’s 
reaction function helps markets better anticipate policy 
changes and quickly incorporate new information into financial 
conditions, thereby accelerating monetary policy transmission 
to the economy. So, he believes that unwinding that 
transparency would be a mistake. (Note: GS Global 
Economists Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters lay out the 
evidence backing this argument on pgs. 14-15.) 
But Hatzius takes a more nuanced view of forward guidance, 
arguing that rigid “Odyssean” guidance that commits 
policymakers to a pre-set path regardless of new information 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, while conditional “Delphic” guidance tied to the 
economic outlook is generally more defensible. Although 
markets could confuse the two, he believes that most 
sophisticated investors understand the difference and is 
therefore not particularly worried that markets have become 
overly reliant on the policy dots published in the Fed’s 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP). Instead, he 
considers them a “useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function” (except for the long-run 
policy dot). If anything, Hatzius would prefer the dots move 
in an even more informative direction—by publishing each 
policymaker’s combined projections for growth, inflation, 
unemployment, and the policy rate on an anonymized basis. 
Kohn agrees that clarity about the Fed’s “narrative” and 
reaction function is vital. Explaining how the Fed sees the 
economy evolving and how policy would respond, he says, is 
“essential in helping markets act as a stabilizing force and is 
a core component of accountability.” Still, he sympathizes 
somewhat with Chairman Warsh’s view that the dot plot is 
“not helpful” (see pg. 9 for Warsh’s own words on this), 
arguing that the market’s hyper-focus on the SEP’s median 
policy dot “illustrates the risk of excessive precision and 
false certainty,” as small shifts in it can trigger unwarranted 
market moves. So, he has long favored omitting the median. 
Miran, though, is more supportive of Warsh’s push for a less 
transparent Fed. Like Hatzius and Kohn, he’s concerned that 
excessive forward guidance that locks the Fed into a policy 
path raises the risk of policy errors and also argues that such 
guidance can impair market pricing of risk. But he’s less 
convinced about the benefits of increased transparency 
around the Fed’s reaction function, making the case that the 
distinction between the Fed committing to a pre-set policy 
path and being transparent about its reaction function is clear 
in theory, but less so in practice. So, he thinks “the policy dots 
absolutely have to go” but feels less strongly about omitting 
the SEP’s economic projections. 
But would less transparency allow markets to provide the Fed 
with more “direct and unfiltered” information about the 
economy, as Warsh has argued? The answer seems to boil 
down to the implications for market volatility, and whether any 
resulting volatility would simply increase the market’s noise or 
also improve its signal. 
As for the volatility implications, the evidence is fairly clear. 
Briggs and Peters find that increased central bank 
transparency has led to a reduction in market volatility over the 
last several decades. This is consistent with GS Head of US 
Rates Strategy William Marshall’s recent observation of the 
reverse: that the US rates market has already priced 
substantial uncertainty associated with a less transparent Fed, 
with the July FOMC representing the largest non-cut meeting 
day surprise in almost three decades.  
But Marshall argues that whether such meeting-day surprises 
lead to structurally higher rates volatility depends (again) on 
whether the Fed’s reaction function is well understood. If it’s 
not, he would expect higher rates volatility to persist. GS FX 
Strategists Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter find a similar 
pattern in FX: that central bank communication shifts can 
change which events and data drive FX volatility without 
necessarily raising it, but less transparency could increase 
Dollar volatility—all else equal (though global macro 
conditions remain its biggest driver).  
But the key debate seems to be whether the increased 
volatility resulting from less Fed transparency will improve or 
worsen the market’s signal. Hatzius is clearly in the “worsen” 
camp. He argues that markets price what they think the Fed 
will do—not what it should do—and less transparency won’t 
change that; it would simply produce worse guesses. So, he 
says that while volatility in and of itself is not necessarily bad, 
volatility driven by uncertainty about the Fed’s reaction 
function would serve no constructive economic purpose. 
Kohn agrees that the lack of a clear Fed narrative could create 
damaging uncertainty. But he argues that removing overly 
specific guidance could help free markets to process new 
information more independently and isn’t too worried about 
modestly higher volatility as a result. And Miran seems most 
convinced that reducing transparency would improve the 
markets’ signal, with volatility an unavoidable but acceptable 
cost: “The first best world of signal without volatility doesn't 
exist. I'd rather have that signal, even if it's messy, than 
throw the signal out by anchoring market expectations to the 
Fed's near-term projections.” 
Allison Nathan, Editor
